### #257 2026-10-17 · feature (by hand, the owner's word of 2026-10-17: "what would be an easy way to open more of the same terminal tab? A menu is a bit awkward... Ctrl+click?", then "on the tab"; Feature queue item 1)
- Did: a Ctrl+click on a terminal tab opens the same connection again in a new tab. Main keeps the request of a running session too now (`session.request` in `terminal.js`; until now only an ended session had one, for reconnecting), and `duplicate(id, owner, size)` opens the request of a session of that window again, running or ended (an ended one stays for reconnecting), through the new channel `terminal:duplicate` (preload `terminal.duplicate`). The store's `terminalDuplicate` puts the copy right after the tab (in its row while the panel is split), selects it and gives it only `request: { duplicateOf: id }`, so the page holds no request; `start()` of the panel asks `terminal:duplicate` with that id instead of `terminal:open`; the tab row emits `duplicate` instead of `activate` for a click with Ctrl (`clicked()`; Ctrl+click on the x only closes). A tab that is still opening (also one that connects again: `reopen` takes its request out of `ended` until it answers) or that could not be opened answers with a message instead (`duplicateRefusal`). The Info window has the row *Ctrl+click: On a tab: open the same connection again.*; for that tenth row its key list is a little tighter (0.25rem between the rows instead of 0.3, less space above and below the list), so the window still fits the default window without scrolling. README, CHANGELOG (Added), CLAUDE.md. Commit a408cf2.

- Did: a Ctrl+click on a port forwarding tab that runs opens nothing and says why: *The ports this tab forwards are in use while it runs, so a second tab could not forward them. Open it again once it has ended.* (`duplicateRefusal` in `utils/terminal-sessions.js`). Such a session holds its local ports while it runs, and ssh starts a forward with `ExitOnForwardFailure=yes`, so the copy of #257 would have ended at once with the ports in use; an ended forwarding tab is copied as before, and a tab of any other kind is copied while it runs. ### #266 2026-10-17 · UX (by hand, the owner's word of 2026-10-17 on the candidate found in #264: "I don't know how to fix it. Handle it as you see fit.", Requests (12); how it is fixed was my choice)
- Did: every use of the terminal tab row ends with the keyboard in a terminal. A tab has `tabindex="0"` (Enter and Space select it), so a press on it takes the keyboard, and nothing gave it back where the selected tab did not change: a click, Enter or Space on the selected tab and a refused Ctrl+click (on the selected tab or another one) left it on the tab, a drag inside a row left it on `body` and a drag let go over the canvas on the tab, so what was typed went nowhere (measured on the build before the change with a new probe of the checks, `lists/tab-focus-probe.cjs`). The choice of the tab row now goes to `selectTab()` of the panel, which gives the terminal of the selected tab the keyboard and otherwise selects as before (`activate()` is unchanged: the `focusin` of the screens uses it too); `duplicate()` gives it back to the terminal in front (`focusFront()`) when it refuses; the lists of the tab row send the `end` of Sortable (it comes only for a drag that started, wherever it was let go) as `dragged`, and `dragged()` gives it back to the terminal in front. Found on the way and fixed with it: Enter on a tab that has the keyboard reached the session of the tab it selected (`\r`, measured with the fake preload; in a shell that runs the line typed there, and by the code an ended tab connects again), because the focus moves during the keydown and the keypress follows it; Enter is now `.prevent` like Space. That was so before #266 for another tab (a UI mutation of this run shows it), and the change would have brought it to the selected tab too. A `$nextTick` first put into `dragged()` was taken out again: a UI mutation without it went unseen, and a second probe, `lists/focus-move-probe.cjs`, showed why: a screen that Vue moves into the new order loses the keyboard, but vuedraggable sends `end` in a `$nextTick`, after that redraw; a test reads that from the installed vuedraggable. CLAUDE.md, README, CHANGELOG (Fixed). Commit d15c463.

The tests caught 13 of 13 mutations (a click on the selected tab giving nothing, selecting it again, or only giving the keyboard while the panel is minimized; a refused Ctrl+click giving nothing back, a copy giving the keyboard to the old terminal; the end of a drag giving nothing; the keyboard given to the tab of the other half; the tab row going straight to `activate`; the end of a drag not sent or not heard; Enter not prevented; and in the installed vuedraggable, restored afterwards, the event sent at once or before the list changed). Headless checks: 34 runs passed (1115 checks), with a new `tab-focus-check` (20 checks: where the keyboard is, from `document.activeElement`, and what the sessions are sent, after a click, Enter and Space on the selected tab and on the other one, a copy main could not open, refused Ctrl+clicks on the selected tab and on another one, drags inside a row of the selected tab and of another one, a drag let go over the canvas, a drag into the other row of a split, clicks on the tabs of the halves and on the selected tab of the minimized panel). `duplicate-check` no longer clicks into the screen after its refused Ctrl+click (the workaround of #264): it checks that the keyboard is already there. ### #267 2026-10-18 · UX (by hand, the owner's answer of 2026-10-18 on the candidate found in #116: "like a usual menu" (recommended), Requests (13))
- Did: the right-click menus of the canvas and of a node open like a usual menu. They opened 60px left of the pointer and 20px above it, with the first item under the pointer (*Add node* on the canvas, *Delete* on a node, so a second click there added a node or deleted one), because the margins of the plugin's scoped rule won over ours; at the right and bottom edges the plugin pushed the menu into the window (`fitViewport`), in the top left corner of the canvas it was cut off, and with the terminal panel open a menu right above the panel was half under it (measured on the build before the change with a new probe of the checks, `lists/menu-place-probe.cjs`). The plugin takes a `vueComponent`: `components/editor/context-menu.js` extends its menu. `menuPlace()` of the new `utils/menu-place.js` puts the top left corner 2px right of and below the pointer and turns to the left or upward where the canvas (`#rete`, the parent of the menu, not the window) has no room, so the pointer is always outside the menu at one of its corners; `margin: 0` in the `#rete &` block of `context-menu.scss` takes the plugin's margins away. The plugin's own `updated()` runs first and pushes the menu into the window; ours places it from the pointer remembered in `show()` and then cuts it by the plugin's rule once more, so that both give the same place in the next update (two rules that disagree, in a window smaller than the menu, set the place back and forth without end: with that cut taken out the new check found the page hung). With the pointer outside the menu, a menu nobody went into no longer closed: the plugin closes it when the mouse leaves it after being in it, at an item, and on Rete's `click`, which comes only for a press on `#rete` itself (not on a node, the header or a terminal). So `show()` adds a capture `pointerdown` listener on the document that closes the menu at a press outside it, and `hide()`, which every way of closing goes through, takes it away (the plugin makes a new menu at every right click and never destroys the old ones). A node from the canvas menu still goes where its item is pressed (Rete's `area.mouse`), which is now right of and below the right click; README and CHANGELOG say so. Commit 9fea268.
- Result: tests 1500 passed (new: the place at the pointer, turned at the right and the bottom edge and over a canvas that ends higher, an exact fit and one pixel more, a canvas that does not start at the corner of the window, canvases smaller than the menu, and for every pointer on a grid of the default canvas, with both menus, the menu inside the canvas and the pointer outside it at one of its corners; the menu extends the plugin's, listens while it is open in the capture phase and not after it closed, closes at a press outside it and not at one inside, takes its place from the pointer after the plugin's push, inside the canvas, agrees with the plugin's rule in a window smaller than the menu, does nothing while closed; the editor gives the plugin this menu; and from the installed plugin: `vueComponent` used for both menus, the pointer as `clientX` and `clientY`, `show()` setting `x` and `y`, the style drawing them, its `updated()` with `fitViewport` and the rule of that, every way of closing going through `hide()`, and its margins, which `context-menu.scss` takes away), lint ok, build ok. The tests caught 29 of 29 mutations (in `menu-place.js`: no gap, no turn, a turned menu without the gap, the room test without the gap, a turn past the start of the canvas, a small canvas pushed past its start, the width against the height; in `context-menu.js`: not the plugin's menu, the pointer not remembered, the plugin's `show()` or `hide()` not called, no listener, a listener in the bubbling phase, a listener left after closing, a press inside closing it, a press outside not closing it, the size without the border, the window as the room, `x` or `y` not cut by the plugin's rule, a place while closed; the editor without the menu; the style keeping the plugin's margins or a margin of its own outside `#rete`; and in the installed plugin, restored afterwards: its menus without the option, another push into the window, its `updated()` placing by another element, its menu closing by another method, an item closing only itself). Two of the patterns pinned at first were also in the source map inside the build; they are pinned by the compiled code now. Headless checks: 35 runs passed (1137 checks), with a new `menu-check` (22 checks: the canvas and the node menu at the pointer, whole and drawn, nothing under the pointer; a click at the place of the right click adds or deletes nothing; the right edge, the bottom, the bottom right and the top left corner; above the open terminal panel; closed by a press on a node, in the header and into the terminal, left open by a mouse that only moves away, closed by itself after the mouse went in and left; the capture listeners of the document counted over CDP while a menu is open, after it closed and after menus opened over each other; *Add node*; a right click in a window of 200 x 140, smaller than the menu). The first full run had failed only in `menu-check`, at "no errors or warnings on the page": xterm.js had printed `task queue exceeded allotted deadline by 161ms` (its `TaskQueue` warns when an idle task of its own runs more than 20 ms past the deadline the browser gave it; 2 of 4 runs of the check back to back, after its terminal opened; the other checks with terminals count errors only), so the check counts every warning but that one now. They caught 11 of 11 mutations of the renderer (the plugin's own menu, the plugin's margins, no gap, no turn, the size without the border, the window as the room, no listener, a listener in the bubbling phase, a listener left after closing, a press inside closing it, no cut by the plugin's rule, which hung the page). The other checks open the menu and press its items by name and passed unchanged. No real window: main and the preload are not changed.

### #271 2026-10-18 · feature (by hand, the owner's request of 2026-10-18 after #270: "something has to be done about tmux: in tmux it is enough that copy and paste and the mouse wheel work well", Requests (18))
- Did: asked first what the wheel does in the owner's tmux: "the screen scrolls up", so that tmux has `mouse on` and gets the wheel (xterm.js sends it as mouse reports; without `mouse on` it would be the arrow keys, the alternate screen has no scrollback). What was missing is the copy: with `mouse on` a drag in tmux selects in tmux and copies into its own buffer, and tmux hands the text to the terminal as OSC 52 (`ESC ] 52 ; ; base64 BEL`, with its default `set-clipboard external`; TERM is `xterm-256color`), which the terminal of the app ignored, so nothing reached the Windows clipboard. Measured first that it arrives (`lists/osc52-probe.cjs` of the checks, new; `fake-ssh-server.cjs` hands probes the streams of its shells): from the throwaway SSH server through Git's ssh and the bundled ConPTY to the output of the app's terminal manager, every OSC 52 came byte for byte (BEL and ST, an empty `Pc` and `c`, Korean in UTF-8, 100 KB and 1 MB of base64, 1 MB in about 1.2 s), and so did the same as OSC 5252; a query got no answer on the way (the server counted no byte back and kept nothing). The owner allowed the clipboard to change once to the marker text of the probe; the probe read only the change number of the clipboard, never its content, and would have sent no more OSC 52 after a change, but that number read 0 throughout in the shell of this session (in its PowerShell too: no access to the clipboard there), so whether a layer on the way also set the clipboard is not known (it would be the same text). Then: the panel registers a handler for OSC 52 (`CLIPBOARD_OSC`) next to the login marker and writes what `oscCopyText()` of `utils/terminal-sessions.js` gives through `clipboard:writeText`: the part after the first `;` read as base64 and decoded as UTF-8 (a byte order mark at the start dropped), and nothing for a query (`?`, an answer would hand the clipboard of the PC to the server), an empty text, broken base64, a sequence without its clipboard part (xterm empties the clipboard there; here what was copied stays) or a text with a control character other than tab, LF and CR (pasted into a shell, `ESC [ 201 ~` would end the bracketed paste and the rest would run; tmux copies the text of the screen, which has none; my choice, after the review). The clipboard part itself is not looked at (Windows has one). Every tab writes, also one not in front or of a space that is not open (the program does not know whether its tab is in front; my choice). The ended line of a tab (`endedLine()` of `utils/terminal-view.js`) starts with CAN, so that a copy cut off by the end of the connection is dropped and not copied in part. No new channel; main and the preload are not changed. The owner then asked whether somebody had already made a tool for that: `@xterm/addon-clipboard` 0.2.0, the addon of xterm.js for OSC 52 and the release that goes with xterm.js 6.0.0, was downloaded with the owner's permission and read: its default provider writes only for `Pc` `c` (a copy of tmux, with an empty `Pc`, would not arrive), it answers a query with the clipboard of the PC, and broken or unpadded base64 empties the clipboard; with a provider of our own it would save the split and the decoding and still answer every query (with nothing), for two more packages (it needs `js-base64`). So the panel keeps its own handler (CLAUDE.md says why). README (a paragraph *tmux*: `mouse on`, the drag, Ctrl+V up to 64 KB or Shift with a right click, `set-clipboard`, Shift with a drag for the terminal's own selection, the wheel without `mouse on`, write only, no control characters, about 7 MB at most), CHANGELOG (Added), CLAUDE.md. Commit 833255a.

The tests caught 18 of 18 mutations (no `;` needed, split at the last `;`, only an empty clipboard part, Latin-1 instead of UTF-8, the byte order mark kept, an empty text written, control characters taken, only ESC refused, tabs and line ends refused too, a lenient base64 decoder, another number, the ended line without CAN; in the panel: no handler, nothing written, the sequence written instead of its text, only the tab in front, the handler saying it did not handle it, an answer to a query). Headless checks: 37 runs passed (1177 checks), with the new `osc52-check` (19 checks): the real xterm.js reads what the fake session prints, and the fake `clipboard:writeText` gets the text of the form of tmux, of `c` with ST, in UTF-8 with a new line, with tabs, line ends and spaces at both ends as they were, of a sequence in pieces (also between the ESC and the backslash of ST) and of one inside output (the output is shown, the sequence is not); a query is not answered (nothing goes to the session, the clipboard is not read), an empty text, broken base64, a sequence without its clipboard part and a text with control characters (`ESC[201~`, a bell, a NUL, Ctrl+C) copy nothing; 750 KB in one piece and in seven, 7 499 997 characters whole (9 999 996 of base64, at the limit of xterm.js; about 1.3 s from the write to the copy, of which xterm.js reading it is about 0.7 s) and 7 500 000 not, with the next copy still coming; a tab not in front and a tab of a space that is not open copy too; a copy cut off by the end of the session copies nothing, and the ended line is shown. They caught 8 of 8 mutations of the renderer (no handler, Latin-1, the sequence written, only the tab in front, an answer to a query, a lenient decoder, control characters taken, the ended line without CAN). A review of the change by a second reader (the decoding, the handler in the life of a tab, the claims of the docs against the installed xterm.js, the tests), with its findings weighed by an advisor: what it found was taken in before the commit. A text with control characters went to the clipboard as it came, and pasted into a shell `ESC [ 201 ~` in it would have ended the bracketed paste (`paste()` of xterm.js strips nothing, read in the installed one); a connection that ended in the middle of an OSC 52 left xterm.js in it, and the ESC of the ended line ended it as a whole one, so the part that had come could be copied (now a CAN in front of that line); the bytes were made with `Uint8Array.from(binary, fn)`, which took 0.8 to 1 s for 7.5 MB in Node (a loop: 20 to 40 ms); a byte order mark at the start was dropped by accident and is now dropped on purpose, and the test wrote the mark and U+FFFD as the characters themselves (the `\u` escapes had become them on the way into the file), so they are made with `String.fromCharCode`; no test kept spaces at both ends and a new line at the end; README and CHANGELOG said that Ctrl+V pastes the copy in the app, which is so up to 64 KB. Its findings on the order of the handlers, the life of a tab, unhandled promises and the claims about Ctrl+V, Shift with a right click, Shift with a drag, the wheel and the limit of 10 000 000 characters came back clean. Main and the preload are not changed, so the real window was not run (the probe went through the real pty and ssh). CI run 39121896346 green.

## Routine

- **Local routine since 2026-10-10 (owner: "every 40 minutes, tests, finding and fixing bugs, maintenance").** Two scheduled tasks of the Claude desktop app on the owner's PC, `jumpspace-maintenance-a` (cron `0,40 */2 * * *`) and `jumpspace-maintenance-b` (cron `20 1-23/2 * * *`, local time), together one run every 40 minutes. Each run is a fresh session; this file is its only memory. They run only while the app is open. The cloud trigger below is the old one and stays disabled.
- **Stopped (both tasks disabled, not deleted) on 2026-10-10 at the owner's request**: working on the owner's own PC got in the way (test windows of the app and error dialogs appeared while the owner was using it). The two runs that fired before that left no commit and no log entry. Everything up to #101 was pushed by hand on the owner's word. Do not start the tasks again unless the owner asks; if they do, the "On the owner's PC" points in the Protocol still apply.
- **Those two tasks no longer exist** (2026-10-11: the list of scheduled tasks on this PC was empty and their folder was gone, so what their two runs did cannot be looked up). The likely reason they left nothing: a run pauses at the first tool it has no approval for, and approvals are given per task, during a run.
- **Started again on 2026-10-11 at the owner's request** ("before building the installer go on in a 40-minute cycle with tests, raising the completeness, refactoring and the like"), as two new scheduled tasks of the Claude desktop app: `jumpspace-routine-a` (cron `7,47 */2 * * *`) and `jumpspace-routine-b` (cron `19 1-23/2 * * *`, local time), together one run every 40 minutes. The app starts each task a fixed time after its cron minute, and not the same for both (a about 5 minutes, b about 13), so the minutes are not 20 apart: the runs start at about :12 and :52 of the even hours and :32 of the odd ones. Each run is a fresh session with this file as its memory, and they run only while the app is open. What is different from 2026-10-10: no window is started (headless checks, see the Protocol), every run pushes, and the installer waits for the owner's word. The tool approvals of a task are remembered from its runs, so the first run of each task has to be watched (or started with *Run now* by the owner) until it has what it needs. To stop: disable both tasks (`update_scheduled_task` with `enabled: false`, or the switch in the app's *Scheduled* list).
- **Both tasks disabled the same evening (2026-10-11), at the owner's word ("switch").** The first run (#125) stopped at the permission prompt of its first shell command until the owner approved it by hand, and every task needs that again for every kind of tool. The routine now runs inside the owner's conversation with Claude, as a 40-minute cron of that session (runs at about :07 and :47 of the even hours and :27 of the odd ones): no permission prompts and the result shows there, but it ends when that session is closed and expires after 7 days. The Protocol is the same. The owner also said to keep the log entries short: the template, three lines.
- **How the cycle ends (owner, 2026-10-13: "go on, and when you think there is nothing more to do, build the executable and stop the scheduler").** Nothing more to do means: CI green, no known bug, the Feature queue empty, and two runs in a row that found nothing to fix. Then the run that sees it starts the installer build (`gh workflow run ci.yml --ref claude/cool-bardeen-9x9ymz`), checks that the run succeeded and has the artifact `jumpspace-windows-installer`, deletes the session timers, logs it and tells the owner how to get the file. Until then no installer is built.

- **How the cycle ends, second time (owner, 2026-10-13: "now only raise the tests and the completeness, go as far as you can, then end the scheduler"; #192).** A run now does only tests and completeness: the top item of the Wind-down list in the Backlog, and when that list is empty one failure case of its own choice. No feature, refactor or style runs; a bug that is found is fixed in that run. It ends when the list is empty, CI is green, no bug is known but the one the owner left (#107), and two runs in a row had nothing to add or fix: that run deletes the two session timers (`7,47 */2` and `27 1-23/2`, set again in #192 with this text), writes it here and in the memory file and reports. No installer this time: the owner did not ask for one. **Added the same evening (#193), the owner: "when there seems to be nothing more to improve, push and merge, if possible; in any case put it on git", and "turn the scheduler off some day".** So the run that ends the cycle also brings the default branch up to date, in this order: the last log entry and the note here, committed; the branch pushed and its CI green; `develop` moved forward to that commit with a plain push (`git push origin claude/cool-bardeen-9x9ymz:develop`; `develop` has no commit the branch lacks, so it is a fast-forward: no pull request, no squash, no force; if the push is refused, leave it and report); the CI run of `develop` looked at; the two timers deleted; the memory file; the report. `master`, the tags, the version and the installer are not touched: merging into `develop` is not a release. And the cycle ends in any case with the run that writes entry #205, whatever is left on the list then. Of the two timers only the one at :27 of the odd hours ends the cycle (the session's permission check refused a second timer that had the push to `develop` in its text); a run of the other one (:07 and :47 of the even hours) that sees the end condition only writes that down.

- **The cycle ended on 2026-10-14 (#206), by the rule above: at the latest with the run that writes #205.** #205 was the last run with a work item, and it was a run of the :07/:47 timer, so the next run of the :27 timer ended the cycle, with #206 as its entry and no work item. Its steps after the commit of that entry: the branch pushed and its CI looked at, `develop` moved forward to that commit with a plain push (no pull request, no squash, no force) and its CI looked at, both session timers deleted, the memory file, the report to the owner. This note is part of the commit those steps move, so it is written before them: `git log origin/develop` shows whether the push went through, and the report says how each step ended. Left on the Wind-down list: items 6 and 7. No installer was built, and `master`, the tags and the version were not touched. Nothing runs by itself now; start it again only on the owner's word.

- **Started again on 2026-10-14 (#218) at the owner's word** ("turn the scheduler on at 40 minutes; after tests, refactoring and a completeness review, when there seems to be nothing left to do, build the installer, stop the scheduler and wait"): two session timers as before (`7,47 */2` and `27 1-23/2`, local time; they end with the session and after 7 days), both with the same text. A run does one of: `tests` (behavior without a test, then a mutation check), `refactor` (a code hot spot; behavior unchanged, tests green without edits) or a completeness review (one part of the app or one failure case: a broken file, a race, wrong input, a limit, docs that no longer match the code), the one done least recently first; a bug found is fixed in that run. **It ends** when CI is green, no bug is known but #107, and two runs in a row had nothing to add or fix. That run deletes both timers first (`CronList` empty), runs every check on HEAD (`real-window-check.cjs`, `real-window-check-2.cjs` with all parts, the headless checks), starts the installer build on the work branch (`gh workflow run ci.yml --ref claude/cool-bardeen-9x9ymz`), waits until that run is completed and green with the artifact `jumpspace-windows-installer`, writes it here and in the memory file, pushes, reports to the owner and waits. `develop`, `master`, the tags and the version are not touched.
